R=R_{S,sg}\in(0,1)

such that the following properties hold:

(1)

S
is
d
–Ahlfors regular with constant
\widetilde{C_{0}}
and
S
is
d
–rectifiable (see
(H_{1})
and
(H_{3})
) ;

(2)

\displaystyle S_{sg}=\bigcup_{l=0}^{d-1}S_{sg,l}
and for each
0\leq l\leq d-1
,
S_{sg,l}
is closed and
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|S_{sg,l}}
is
l
–Ahlfors regular:

(
H_{4}
)

\forall x\in S_{sg,l}\text{ and }0<r\leq R,\quad C^{-1}r^{l}\leq\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,r))\leq Cr^{l}\>;

(3)

for all
0<r\leq R
and for all
x\in S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr}
,
S\cap B(x,r)
is a
{\rm C}^{1,a}
graph: there exist an open neighborhood
\mathcal{U}
of
0
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
u:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n-d}
of class
{\rm C}^{1,a}
such that
u(0)=0
and

(
H_{5}
)

(id,u):\mathcal{U}\rightarrow q(S\cap B(x,r))\text{ is a diffeomorphism and,}\quad\forall y,z\in\mathcal{U},\,\|Du(z)-Du(y)\|\leq C|z-y|^{a}\>,

where
q
is an affine isometry sending the affine tangent plane
x+T_{x}M
to
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}^{n-d}
.

Remark 6.3

(Uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{m,a}
sets)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
m\geq 1
and
0<a\leq 1
. Though we are not addressing higher regularity issue, note that it is also possible to define

(1)

uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{m}
sets: replacing
{\rm C}^{1,a}
with
{\rm C}^{m}
in (

H
5

) and dropping the Hölder condition on
Du
,

(2)

uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{m,a}
sets: replacing
{\rm C}^{1,a}
with
{\rm C}^{m,a}
in (

H
5

) and writing the Hölder condition on
D^{m}u
instead of
Du
.

Example 6.4

(Submanifold with boundary)
.

Note that with the above definition of uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{1,a}
set
S
, if
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a
d
–submanifold with boundary then such a boundary necessarily lies in the singular set
S_{sg}
(this is more precisely due to (

H
5

)) and the decomposition of the singular set
S_{sg}
amounts to
S_{sg}=S_{sg,d-1}
, that is exactly the boundary of
S
, all the other
S_{sg,l}
being empty. As we just mention, the sets
S_{sg,l}
can be empty and are not required to be disjoint.

Definition 6.5
.

Let
0<b\leq 1
, let
S
be a closed set satisfying
\mathcal{H}^{d}(S)<\infty
and let
\theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a Borel function. We say that
\theta
is uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{0,b}
if there exist
a closed set
\Theta_{sg}\subset S
,
C=C_{\theta,sg}\geq 1
and
R=R_{\theta,sg}\in(0,1)

such that the following properties hold:

(1)

\theta\in{\rm L}^{1}(\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S})
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\theta\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}=1
and there exist
0<\theta_{min}\leq\theta_{max}<+\infty
such that for
\mathcal{H}^{d}
–a. e.
x\in S
,

\theta_{min}\leq\theta(x)\leq\theta_{max}\>.

(see
(H_{2})
) ;

(2)

\displaystyle\Theta_{sg}=\bigcup_{l=0}^{d-1}\Theta_{sg,l}
and for each
0\leq l\leq d-1
,
\Theta_{sg,l}
is closed and
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|\Theta_{sg,l}}
is
l
–Ahlfors regular:

(
H_{6}
)

\forall x\in\Theta_{sg,l}\text{ and }0<r\leq R,\quad C^{-1}r^{l}\leq\mathcal{H}^{l}(\Theta_{sg,l}\cap B(x,r))\leq Cr^{l}\>;

(3)

for all
0<r\leq R
and for all
x\in S\setminus(\Theta_{sg})^{Cr}
,
\theta
is
{\rm C}^{0,b}
in
S\cap B(x,r)
:

(
H_{7}
)

\forall y,z\in S\cap B(x,r),\,|\theta(z)-\theta(y)|\leq C|z-y|^{b}\>.

Note that though we shortened the terminology, Definition
6.5
above does not tell anything about
\theta
outside
S
and it would be more accurate to say that
\theta
is uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{0,b}
with respect to
S
.

Remark 6.6

(Constant
M
)
.

All along the current section (Section
6
),
M
stands for a generic constant that may vary from one statement to another one, and only depends on
d,n,\widetilde{C_{0}},\eta,\varphi,\theta_{min/max},C=\max(C_{\theta,sg},C_{S,sg})\geq 1,\>R=\min(R_{\theta,sg},R_{S,sg})<1
and is consequently uniform in the regularity class
\mathcal{P}
. If relevant, a more restrictive dependency can be indicated in some statements.

We will use the following notations for the unions of singular sets:

(101)

\mathfrak{S}=S_{sg}\cup\Theta_{sg}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{S}_{l}=S_{sg,l}\cup\Theta_{sg,l}\>.

The following Lemma
6.7
draws consequences of (

H
4

) and (

H
6

) concerning the structure of the singular set: each set
\mathfrak{S}_{l}
is
l
–Ahlfors regular and the
\mathcal{H}^{d}
measure of a
\rho
–neighbourhood
\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}
in
S
of
\mathfrak{S}_{l}
behaves like
\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l})
times the thickness
\rho^{d-l}
where the exponent
d-l
corresponds to the co-dimension of
\mathfrak{S}_{l}
relatively to
S
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
0<a,b\leq 1
.
We assume that
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{1,a}
in the sense of Definition
6.2
and
\theta:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
is uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{0,b}
in the sense of Definition
6.5
. In other words,
S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

). Then

(1)

Let
0<\rho\leq R
and
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a Borel set, then for
0\leq l\leq d-1
,

(102)

\mathcal{H}^{d}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\cap S)\leq M\rho^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2\rho})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{d}(\mathfrak{S}^{\rho}\cap D\cap S)\leq M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\rho^{d-l}\>\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2\rho})\>,

and similarly
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d}(S_{sg,l}^{\rho}\cap D\cap S)\leq M\rho^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap D^{2\rho})
and
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d}(\Theta_{sg,l}^{\rho}\cap D\cap S)\leq M\rho^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\Theta_{sg,l}\cap D^{2\rho})
.

(2)

Up to renaming the constants, assumption (

H
4

), respectively (

H
6

), could be equivalently replaced with:

\displaystyle S_{sg}=\bigcup_{l=0}^{d-1}S_{sg,l}
and for each
0\leq l\leq d-1
,
S_{sg,l}
is closed and satisfy: for all
x\in S_{sg,l}
and
0<r\leq R_{B}\leq R
,

(
H_{4}^{\prime}
)

\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B}))\leq CR_{B}^{l}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{d}(S_{sg,l}^{r}\cap B(x,R_{B})\cap S)\leq Cr^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B}))\>,

respectively,

\displaystyle\Theta_{sg}=\bigcup_{l=0}^{d-1}\Theta_{sg,l}
and for each
0\leq l\leq d-1
,
\Theta_{sg,l}
is closed and satisfy: for all
x\in\Theta_{sg,l}
and
0<r\leq R_{B}\leq R
,

(
H_{6}^{\prime}
)

\mathcal{H}^{l}(\Theta_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B}))\leq CR_{B}^{l}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{d}(\Theta_{sg,l}^{r}\cap B(x,R_{B})\cap S)\leq Cr^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\Theta_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B}))

(3)

Let
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open ball of radius
R_{B}\leq\frac{R}{3C}
, and for
0\leq l\leq d-1
, define
\xi_{l}=\xi_{l}(Cr,B)
such that
\xi_{l}=1
if
B\cap(\mathfrak{S}_{l})^{Cr}\neq\emptyset
and
\xi_{l}=0
otherwise. Then for all
0<r\leq R_{B}
,

(103)

\mathcal{H}^{d}((\mathfrak{S}_{l})^{Cr}\cap B\cap S)\leq M\mu(B)\xi_{l}\left(\frac{r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{d}((\mathfrak{S})^{Cr}\cap B\cap S)\leq M\mu(B)\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\>.

Proof.

(1)

Let
0\leq l\leq d-1
,
0<\rho\leq R
and
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a bounded Borel set (we will consider the unbounded case afterwards). Take a family
\mathcal{F}
of two by two disjoint balls with same radius
2\rho
that are centered at
\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D
and satisfy
\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\subset\cup_{B\in\mathcal{F}}2B
(recall that
D
is bounded and one can add disjoint balls as long as possible to obtain such a family
\mathcal{F}
, similarly to the proof of Proposition
2.8
). From the family
\mathcal{F}
we can construct a family
\mathcal{G}
of two by two disjoint balls of radius
\rho
, centered at
\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{\rho}
and such that
\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\subset\cup_{B\in\mathcal{G}}5B
. Indeed, let us write

\mathcal{F}=\left\{B(x_{j},2\rho)\right\}_{j\in J}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{G}=\left\{B(z_{j},\rho)\right\}_{j\in J}\>,

where
z_{j}
is chosen so that
z_{j}\in\mathfrak{S}_{l}
and
|z_{j}-x_{j}|<\rho
(possible since
x_{j}\in\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D
). The ball in
\mathcal{G}
are two by two disjoint: indeed, as the balls in
\mathcal{F}
are disjoint, then for
i\neq j\in J
,
|x_{i}-x_{j}|\geq 4\rho
so that
|z_{i}-z_{j}|>2\rho
. Furthermore,
B(x_{j},4\rho)\subset B(z_{j},5\rho)
and
z_{j}\in\mathfrak{S}_{l}\subset S
so that by
d
–Ahlfors regularity of
\nu=\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}
,
\mathcal{H}^{d}(B(z_{j},5\rho)\cap S)\leq\widetilde{C}_{0}5^{d}\rho^{d}
and then

(104)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\cap S)\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}\left(\cup_{j\in J}B(x_{j},4\rho)\cap S\right)\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}\left(\cup_{j\in J}B(z_{j},5\rho)\cap S\right)\leq 5^{d}\widetilde{C}_{0}(\#J)\rho^{d}\>.

On the other hand,
x_{j}\in D
so that
z_{j}\in D^{\rho}
and thus
B(z_{j},\rho)\subset D^{2\rho}
. By
l
–Ahlfors regularity of
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|\mathfrak{S}_{l}}
,

(105)

(\#J)C^{-1}\rho^{l}\leq\sum_{j\in J}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap B(z_{j},\rho))=\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap\cup_{j\in J}B(z_{j},\rho)\right)\leq\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2\rho}\right)

and combining both estimates (
104
) and (
105
) we infer
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\cap S)\leq 5^{d}\widetilde{C}_{0}C\rho^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2\rho}\right)
. Summing the previous estimates for
l
from
0
to
d-1
, we obtain

\nu\left(\mathfrak{S}^{\rho}\cap D\right)=\nu\left(\cup_{l}\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\right)\leq\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\nu\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\right)\leq M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\rho^{d-l}\>\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2\rho})\>.

Finally, in the case where
D
is not assumed to be bounded, usual non-decreasing measure property leads:

\nu\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap D\right)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\to+\infty\\
k\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\nu\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{\rho}\cap(D\cap B(0,k))\right)\leq M\rho^{d-l}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\to+\infty\\
k\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap(D\cap B(0,k))^{2\rho}\right)\leq M\rho^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2\rho})\>.

The same proof leads to the similar estimates stated for
S_{sg,l}
and
\Theta_{sg,l}
using the
l
–Ahlfors regularity of
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|S_{sg,l}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|\Theta_{sg,l}}
instead of
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|\mathfrak{S}_{l}}
.

(2)

Let us prove that (

H
4

) could be equivalently replaced with (

H
4

′

). First assume that
S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

), let
0\leq l\leq d-1
,
x\in S_{sg,l}
and
0<r\leq R_{B}\leq R
with
R\leq{\rm diam}{S_{sg,l}}
. As
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|S_{sg,l}}
is
l
–Ahlfors regular, we directly have

\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B}))\leq CR_{B}^{l}\>.

Applying
(i)
with
D=B(x,R_{B})
and
\rho=r
, we obtain

(106)

\mathcal{H}^{d}(S_{sg,l}^{r}\cap B(x,R_{B})\cap S)\leq Mr^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B}+2r))\leq Mr^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B}))\>,

where the last inequality follows from
R_{B}+2r\leq 3R_{B}
and the
l
–Ahlfors regularity of
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|S_{sg,l}}
.

Conversely assume that
S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to
(H_{3})
, (

H
4

′

), (

H
5

) to (

H
7

). Let
0\leq l\leq d-1
,
x\in S_{sg,l}
and
0<R_{B}\leq R
, note that
S_{sg,l}
being closed,
\supp\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|S_{sg,l}}=S_{sg,l}
. By (

H
4

′

) we already have
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B})\right)\leq CR_{B}^{l}
.
Then, taking
r=R_{B}
in (

H
4

), we have
S_{sg,l}^{r}\cap B(x,R_{B})=B(x,R_{B})
so that

R_{B}^{d}\leq\widetilde{C_{0}}\mathcal{H}^{d}\left(B(x,R_{B})\cap S\right)\leq\widetilde{C_{0}}CR_{B}^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B})\right)\\
\quad\Rightarrow\quad(C\widetilde{C_{0}})^{-1}R_{B}^{l}\leq\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B})\right)\>.

We infer that for all
x\in S_{sg,l}
and
0<R_{B}\leq R
,

\displaystyle M^{-1}R_{B}^{l}\leq\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,R_{B})\right)\leq MR_{B}^{l}\>.

We then recall that
S
and thus
S_{sg,l}\subset S
are bounded (see Remark
3.1
) so that it is possible to cover
S_{sg,l}
with a finite number of balls of radius less than
R
centered at
S_{sg,l}
leading to
\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l})<\infty
. We can then conclude that
\mathcal{H}^{l}_{|S_{sg,l}}
is
l
–Ahlfors regular thanks to Remark
2.6
.

The proof for (

H
6

)–(

H
6

′

) is identical.

(3)

Let
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open ball of radius
R_{B}\leq\frac{R}{3C}
and let
0<r\leq R_{B}
. If
(\mathfrak{S}_{l})^{Cr}\cap B=\emptyset
, there is nothing to check, and otherwise, let
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{l}
such that
B\subset B(x,2R_{B}+Cr)\subset B(x,3CR_{B})
(since
C\geq 1
by Definitions
6.2
and
6.5
). Then by
(ii)
(

H
4

′

) and (

H
6

′

), since
Cr\leq CR_{B}\leq 3CR_{B}\leq R
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{d}((\mathfrak{S}_{l})^{Cr}\cap B\cap S)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}((\mathfrak{S}_{l})^{Cr}\cap B(x,3CR_{B})\cap S)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}((S_{sg,l})^{Cr}\cap B(x,3CR_{B})\cap S)+\mathcal{H}^{d}((\Theta_{sg,l})^{Cr}\cap B(x,3CR_{B})\cap S)

\displaystyle\leq C(Cr)^{d-l}\left(\mathcal{H}^{l}(S_{sg,l}\cap B(x,3CR_{B}))+\mathcal{H}^{l}(\Theta_{sg,l}\cap B(x,3CR_{B}))\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C^{2+d}3^{l}r^{d-l}R_{B}^{l}

\displaystyle\leq 2C^{2+d}3^{l}C_{0}\mu(B)\left(\frac{r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\>.

∎

For the sake of clarity, let us give examples that illustrate Definitions
6.2
and
6.5
, focusing on assumptions (

H
4

) to (

H
7

).

Example 6.8
.

Besides smooth or
{\rm C}^{1,a}
compact
d
–submanifolds, natural examples of uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{1,a}
sets are polytopes like the cube for which
\mathfrak{S}_{0}=\{\rm corners\}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{1}=\{\rm edges\}
. Another interesting example is the so–called stadium obtained by gluing two half–circles with two segments. Because of the four gluing points, the stadium is not
{\rm C}^{2}
but only
{\rm C}^{1,1}
, and more important, it is actually uniformly piecewise smooth (in the sense of Definition
6.2
where
{\rm C}^{1,a}
is replaced with smooth in (

H
5

), see Remark
6.3
) with
\mathfrak{S}=\mathfrak{S}_{0}=\{\text{four gluing points}\}
.
The stadium example raises the question of examples of
{\rm C}^{1,a}
–sets that are not uniformly piecewise smooth (loosely speaking, examples of sets that are not smooth or
{\rm C}^{k}
up to a gluing set that can be included in the singular set
\mathfrak{S}
). One can for instance consider primitives of the following family of Weierstrass functions: given
0<s<1
and
t>1
such that
st\geq 1
, define for
x\in[0,1]
,

f_{s,t}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}s^{j}\cos(t^{j}x)\>.

Let
a=-\frac{\ln s}{\ln t}\in(0,1)
. The function
f_{s,t}
is
a
–Hölder though nowhere differentiable (see
[
Har16
]
), whence not Lipschitz according to Rademacher theorem. Then considering a primitive
F_{s,t}
of
f_{s,t}
, the graph of
F_{s,t}
is a
{\rm C}^{1,a}
curve that is not uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{2}
, note that it is
1
–Ahlfors since
F_{s,t}
is Lipschitz in
[0,1]
(see Example
2.7
).

(a)

A stadium.

(b)

f_{s,t}
for
s=0.3
and
t=4
,
a\approx 1.095
.

(c)

F_{s,t}
for
s=0.3
and
t=4
,
a\approx 1.095
.

Regarding the singular set
S_{sg}
, it can be constituted of several dimensional pieces as for simplices and polytopes. However, note that cusps or tangential contacts are not allowed by the local graph property (

H
5

). We believe that there is some room for improvement there since the tangent space does not vary badly near such singularities, such graph requirement might be relaxed. Note also that transverse crossings are allowed and, loosely speaking at least, the maximal angle at crossings is related to the constant
C\geq 1
appearing in
S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr}
in (

H
5

), we refer to Remark
6.9
and Figure
2
below for additional comments regarding
S_{sg}
and (

H
5

).
However,
S_{sg}
itself can be fairly irregular:
for instance considering a bounded open domain
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that
\partial D
is
1
–Ahlfors regular and
S=\overline{D}\times\{0\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
, then
S
is piecewise
{\rm C}^{1,a}
/smooth regular with
S_{sg}=\partial D\times\{0\}
. A similar example regarding the singular set
\Theta_{sg}
is given by

\theta=1+\mathds{1}_{D}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
then
\theta
is piecewise constant outside
\Theta_{sg}=\partial D
and thus uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{0,b}
/smooth with
\Theta_{sg}=\partial D
(with
\partial D

1
–Ahlfors regular), in this example one can consider any compact set
S
containing
D
.

(a)

A transverse contact.

(b)

A tangential contact

Figure 2
.

Remark 6.9

(Possible extensions of such a framework)
.

It would be possible to further relax assumptions on the singular set, such changes would then affect the convergence rate of the
d
–dimensional measure of the ”bad” set in which the pointwise estimates do not hold (i.e. the enlargements of
\mathfrak{S}
).

(1)

First, we chose to allow a singular set
\mathfrak{S}
constituted of a finite union of Ahlfors regular sets
\mathfrak{S}_{l}
of integer dimension
l<d
, however, it would not be difficult to adapt Section
6
and
7
to the case where
\mathfrak{S}
is constituted of a finite union of Ahlfors regular sets
\mathfrak{S}_{l}
of
real
dimension
l\leq d-1
,
l\in\{l_{1},\ldots,l_{J}\}\subset[0,d-1]
. For instance, in Theorem
7.4
, the statement would be unchanged up to summing over real numbers
l_{j}
(
j=1,\ldots,J
) instead of summing over integers
l=0,\ldots,d-1
.
However, the integrality of
l
is not crucial but most natural examples already fit such a framework and we decided to keep the singular strata of integer dimensions.

(2)

As already mentioned, (

H
5

) in particular controls the maximal angle that is allowed around
S_{sg}
. In the particular case illustrated in Figure
2
(a)
, we observe that the constant
C
appearing in
S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr}
(

H
5

) and the contact angle
\theta
at
S_{sg}
then satisfy
C\sin\frac{\theta}{2}>1
thus preventing
\theta\in[0,\pi)
to be smaller then some threshold. Similarly, (

H
5

) excludes tangential contact for
S
. Indeed, considering the following simple example
S
made of a tangential contact between an horizontal line and a parabola as illustrated in Figure
2
(b)
, we observe that the issue stems from the small distance between both branches of
S
, which is no longer linear but quadratic with respect to the distance to the contact point
r
. It is then hopeless to require that
S
is a graph in a ball of radius
r
when only at distance
Cr
from the contact point
S_{sg}
, nevertheless it can be required for point at distance larger than
C\sqrt{r}
from
S_{sg}
as illustrated in Figure
2
in the specific case at hand. More generally, asking the graph requirement to hold for
x\in S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr^{\gamma}}
instead of
S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr}
(for some
\gamma\in(0,1]
) would allow tangential contact at various orders. Under such a modified assumption, Lemma
6.7
(iii)
has then to be adapted to check that in a ball
B
of radius
R_{B}\geq r
the bad set
(S_{sg})^{Cr^{\gamma}}\cap S\cap B
has
d
–dimensional measure controlled by

M\mu(B)\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}(Cr^{\gamma},B)\left(\frac{r^{\gamma}}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\>.

and the analogous change can be straightforwardly transferred to the conclusion of Theorem
7.4
. We note that for small
\gamma
, the convergence rate can be degraded: one can compare
\delta_{N}^{\min(a,b)}
with
\delta_{N}^{\gamma(d-l)}
for
l
such that
\mathfrak{S}_{l}\neq\emptyset
in (
7.4
). Though such an adaptation of the framework is possible, we believe that the convergence rate of Theorem
7.4
should not be generally degraded in presence of tangential contact. Indeed, on one hand, the tangent space estimation should not be degraded around such points since it has small variation. On the other hand, the pointwise estimation of density should give a multiple of the correct density, which is why the pointwise density estimation would fail. However, in our previous example, we emphasize that we would estimate a half–line with density
2
, which is indeed close to the set
S
(near the contact point) when considering the bounded Lipschitz distance, despite the failure of the pointwise estimation of the density. It is not clear to us how to rigorously implement such ideas and we leave it for future thoughts.

We henceforth assume that
S
and
\theta
satisfy
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) up to the end of the paper or equivalently that
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
.

6.2.
Uniform convergence rates for the regularized density
\theta_{\delta}
and covariance matrix
\Sigma_{r}

We now prove that in the regularity class
\mathcal{P}
(see (
100
)) defined in the previous Section
6.1
for
S
and
\theta
, it is possible to obtain uniform convergence rates for the pointwise convergences concerning the density
\theta_{\delta}(x)\xrightarrow[\delta\to 0]{}\theta(x)
and concerning the tangent space
\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu),\>\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)\xrightarrow[\delta,r\to 0]{}\Pi_{T_{x}S}
, provided that
x\in S
is away from the singular set
S_{sg}\cup\Theta_{sg}
, as stated in Proposition
6.11
. We first prove Lemma
6.10
which allows one to deal with both the density and the tangent space approximations as they are both kernel (convolution) based.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
0<a\leq 1
. Let
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a closed set satisfying
\mathcal{H}^{d}(S)<\infty
and assume that
S
is uniformly piecewise
{\rm C}^{1,a}
. Using the notations of Definition
6.2
, let
0<r\leq R=R_{S,sg}
and
x\in S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr}
. Then,
for all
f\in{\rm C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R})
bounded and Lipschitz with support in
B(0,1)
,

\left|\int_{S}f\left(\frac{z-x}{r}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(z)-\int_{x+T_{x}S}f\left(\frac{z-x}{r}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(z)\right|\leq M(\|f\|_{\infty}+{\rm Lip}(f))r^{d+a}\>,

where
M
only depends on
d
and
C_{S,sg}
.

Proof.

Let
0<r\leq R=R_{S,sg}
and
x\in S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr}
. Let
q
be an affine isometry sending
x+T_{x}M
to
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}
and let
u:\mathcal{U}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow q(S\cap B(x,r))
be
{\rm C}^{1,a}
as in
(H_{5})
.
Note that
\mathcal{U}\subset B(0,r)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is necessarily bounded (for
y\in\mathcal{U}
and
z=(y,u(y))
, we have
|y|^{2}\leq|z|^{2}<r^{2}
).
Up to the affine isometry
q
, we suppose that
x=0
and
T_{x}S
is
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}^{n-d}
so that
u(0)=0
and
Du(0)=0
.
Let
\varepsilon>0
such that
B(0,\varepsilon)\subset\mathcal{U}
, then for any
y\in B(0,\varepsilon)
,

(107)

\|Du(y)\|=\|Du(y)-Du(0)\|\leq C|y|^{a}\>,

where
C=C_{S,sg}
, and

(108)

|u(y)|=\left|u(0)+\int_{0}^{1}Du(ty)y\>dt\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{1}(Du(ty)-Du(0))y\>dt\right|\leq C|y|\int_{0}^{1}|ty|^{a}\>dt\leq C|y|^{a+1}\>.

In particular, for
z=(y,u(y))
,
|z|^{2}\leq|y|^{2}\left(1+C^{2}|y|^{2a}\right)
.
Let
\varepsilon_{0}=\min\{|y|\>:\>y\in\partial\mathcal{U}\}
and let
y_{0}\in\partial\mathcal{U}
such that
|y_{0}|=\varepsilon_{0}
. Let us check that

(109)

\varepsilon_{0}\geq r\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+C^{2}r^{2a}}}\>.

Let
(t_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfy
0<t_{k}<1
and
t_{k}\rightarrow 1
, and consider
z_{k}=(t_{k}y_{0},u(t_{k}y_{0}))\in B(x,r)\cap S
: by compactness of
\overline{B(x,r)\cap S}\subset\overline{B(x,r)}\cap S
, we can assume that
z_{k}\rightarrow z=(y_{0},v)\in\overline{B(x,r)}\cap S
. Note that
z\in\partial B(x,r)
otherwise
(y_{0},v)\in B(x,r)\cap S
can be written as
z=(y_{1},u(y_{1}))
with
y_{1}\in\mathcal{U}
incompatible with
y_{1}=y_{0}\in\partial\mathcal{U}
.
By definition of
\varepsilon_{0}
, we have

B(0,\varepsilon_{0})\subset\mathcal{U}
and thus for all
k
,

\underbrace{|t_{k}y_{0}|^{2}+|u(t_{k}y_{0})|^{2}}_{\rightarrow|z|^{2}=r^{2}}\leq|t_{k}y_{0}|^{2}(1+C^{2}|t_{k}y_{0}|^{2a})\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{2}(1+C^{2}r^{2a})\quad\Rightarrow\quad\eqref{eqInnerBallRadius}\>.

Let
f\in{\rm C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R})
be a Bounded Lipschitz function with support in
B(0,1)
. We recall that up to assuming that the affine isometry
q
is the identity map, we have
x=0
,
T_{x}S=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0\}^{n-d}
,
\mathcal{U}\subset B(0,r)
and we apply the area formula to the map
w=({\rm id},u):\mathcal{U}\rightarrow B(x,r)\cap S
in the first integral below:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{z\in S}\right.

\displaystyle\left.f\left(\frac{z-x}{r}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(z)-\int_{z\in x+T_{x}S}f\left(\frac{z-x}{r}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(z)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{y\in\mathcal{U}}f\left(\frac{(y,u(y))}{r}\right)J_{d}w(y)\>dy-\int_{y\in B(0,r)}f\left(\frac{(y,0)}{r}\right)\>dy\right|

(110)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{U}}}_{\int_{B(0,r)}}\left|f\left(\frac{(y,u(y))}{r}\right)J_{d}w(y)-f\left(\frac{(y,0)}{r}\right)\right|\>dy+\int_{B(0,r)\setminus\mathcal{U}}\left|f\left(\frac{(y,0)}{r}\right)\right|\>dy\>,

where
J_{d}w
is the tangential Jacobian of
w=({\rm id},u)
and satisfies (see for instance Remark 2.72 in
[
AFP00
]
)

J_{d}w=\sqrt{1+W_{u}}\quad\text{where}\quad W_{u}(y)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}B\text{ square minor}\\
\text{of }Du(y)\end{subarray}}\det(B)^{2}\>.

Note that for
1\leq k\leq d
,
y\in B(0,r)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
B
a
k\times k
minor of
Du(y)
and reminding (
107
), we have up to multiplicative constants only depending on
d
:

|\det B|\lesssim\|B\|^{k}\lesssim\|Du(y)\|^{k}
and
\|Du(y)\|^{k}\leq C^{k}|y|^{ak}\leq C^{d}r^{a}
since
r^{k}\leq r
and
C^{k}\leq C^{d}
for
r\leq R<1\leq 1
,
C\geq 1
and
1\leq k\leq d
. Therefore, there exists a constant
c(d)\geq 1
only depending of
d
such that

0\leq W_{u}(y)\leq c(d)r^{2a}\>.

Now, by concavity of
\sqrt{1+\cdot}
, for any
t\geq 0
,
\sqrt{1+t}\leq 1+\frac{1}{2}t
and consequently

0\leq J_{d}w(y)-1\leq\frac{1}{2}W_{u}(y)\leq c(d)C^{2d}r^{2a}\quad\Rightarrow\quad|J_{d}w(y)-1|\leq Mr^{2a}\>.

Using
\left|(y,u(y))-(y,0)\right|=|u(y)|\leq C|y|^{a+1}\leq Cr^{a+1}
by (
108
), we can infer that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,r)}\left|f\left(\frac{(y,u(y))}{r}\right)J_{d}w(y)-f\left(\frac{(y,0)}{r}\right)\right|\>dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,r)}\left|f\left(\frac{(y,u(y))}{r}\right)\right|\left|J_{d}w(y)-1\right|\>dy+\int_{B(0,r)}\left|f\left(\frac{(y,u(y))}{r}\right)-f\left(\frac{(y,0)}{r}\right)\right|\>dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty}Mr^{2a}\omega_{d}r^{d}+{\rm Lip}(f)Cr^{a}\omega_{d}r^{d}

(111)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\|f\|_{\infty}+{\rm Lip}(f))Mr^{d+a}\>.

Eventually, we recall that
B(0,\varepsilon_{0})\subset\mathcal{U}
with
\varepsilon_{0}
satisfying (
109
) and we use that for all
t\geq 0
,
(1+t)^{\frac{-d}{2}}\geq 1-\frac{d}{2}t
(follows from convexity argument for instance) so that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,r)\setminus\mathcal{U}}\left|f\left(\frac{(y,0)}{r}\right)\right|\>dy

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{\infty}\left|B(0,r)\setminus B(0,\varepsilon_{0})\right|\leq\|f\|_{\infty}\omega_{d}r^{d}\left(1-(1+C^{2}r^{2a})^{\frac{-d}{2}}\right)

(112)

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{\infty}\omega_{d}r^{d}\frac{d}{2}C^{2}r^{2a}\>.

We conclude the proof of Lemma
6.10
thanks to (
110
), (
111
) and (
112
).
∎

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
0<a,b\leq 1
.
We assume that

S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) i.e
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
. Using the notations of Definitions
6.2
and
6.5
, let
0<\delta,r\leq R=\min(R_{S,sg},R_{\theta,sg})<1
,
C=\max(C_{S,sg},C_{\theta,sg})\geq 1
,
then, with the notation
c=\min(a,b)
,

(113)

\forall x\in S\setminus(\mathfrak{S})^{C\delta},\quad\left|\theta_{\delta}(x)-\theta(x)\right|\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b})\leq M\delta^{c}

and

(114)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}\forall x\in S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr},&\quad\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\leq Mr^{a}\\
\forall x\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{Cr},&\quad\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu)-\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\leq M(r^{a}+r^{b})\leq Mr^{c}\\
\forall x\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C\max(\delta,r)},&\quad\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b}+r^{a}+r^{b})\leq M\left(\delta^{c}+r^{c}\right)\>.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

We recall that
\mathfrak{S}=S_{sg}\cup\Theta_{sg}
and let
0<\delta,r\leq R
.
We start with the proof of (
113
): from (
20
) and then dilation and translation, we have for
x\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}
,

C_{\eta}=\int_{T_{x}S}\eta(|y|)d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)=\frac{1}{\delta^{d}}\int_{x+T_{x}S}\eta\left(\frac{|y-x|}{\delta}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)

so that

\displaystyle\theta_{\delta}(x)-\theta(x)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C_{\eta}\delta^{d}}\int_{B(x,\delta)\cap S}\eta\left(\frac{|y-x|}{\delta}\right)(\theta(y)-\theta(x))\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)

\displaystyle+\theta(x)\frac{1}{C_{\eta}\delta^{d}}\left(\int_{S}\eta\left(\frac{|y-x|}{\delta}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)-\int_{x+T_{x}S}\eta\left(\frac{|y-x|}{\delta}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)\right)\>.

Applying Lemma
6.10
with
f=\eta(|\cdot|)
(that has same Lipschitz and infinity bounds as
\eta
) and thanks to
(H_{1})
and (

H
7

), we can infer

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{\delta}(x)-\theta(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\delta^{b}}{C_{\eta}\delta^{d}}\|\eta\|_{\infty}\underbrace{\mathcal{H}^{d}(B(x,\delta)\cap S)}_{\leq M\delta^{d}}+\theta_{max}\frac{1}{C_{\eta}\delta^{d}}M(\|\eta\|_{\infty}+{\rm Lip}(\eta))\delta^{d+a}

\displaystyle\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b})\>.

We similarly prove (
114
):
by Definition
5.2
and Proposition
5.3
, for
x\in S\setminus(S_{sg})^{Cr}
,

\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|=\frac{1}{C_{\varphi}r^{d}}\left\|\int_{S}\psi\left(\frac{y-x}{r}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)-\int_{x+T_{x}S}\psi\left(\frac{y-x}{r}\right)\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)\right\|\>.

and we infer from Lemma
6.10
with
f=\psi
that

(115)

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{r^{d}}(\|\psi\|_{\infty}+{\rm Lip}(\psi))r^{d+a}\leq Mr^{a}\>.

Then, again thanks to
(H_{1})
and (

H
7

) and (
115
), we obtain if moreover
x\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{Cr}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu)-\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{\varphi}r^{d}}\left\|\int_{S}\psi\left(\frac{y-x}{r}\right)(\theta(y)-\theta(x))\>d\mathcal{H}^{d}(y)\right\|+\theta(x)\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cr^{b}}{C_{\varphi}r^{d}}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{d}(B(x,r)\cap S)+\theta_{max}Mr^{a}

(116)

\displaystyle\leq M(r^{b}+r^{a})\>,

so that finally, from (
114
) and (
116
) we infer: for
x\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C\max(\delta,r)}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta}(x)\right)\left(\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu)-\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right)\right\|+\left\|\left(\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta}(x)\right)-\Phi(\theta(x))\right)\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Phi\|_{\infty}M(r^{b}+r^{a})+\theta_{max}{\rm Lip}(\Phi)M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b})

\displaystyle\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b}+r^{a}+r^{b})\>.

∎

6.3.
Uniform convergence rates of
\nu_{\delta}
and
W_{r,\delta}
in terms of Bounded Lipschitz distance

Still in the same regularity class
\mathcal{P}
(see (
100
)) for
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}
(i.e.
S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) as defined in Section
6.1
), we can draw on the pointwise rates established in Proposition
6.11
to obtain similar rates for
\nu_{\delta}
(as stated in Proposition
6.12
) and
W_{r,\delta}
,
\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta}
(as stated in Proposition
6.13
. The proof simply consists in controlling the measure of offsets of the singular set
S_{sg}\cup\Theta_{sg}
on the one hand and uses the aforementioned pointwise rates on the other hand.

Let us start with uniform bounds for
\|\theta_{\delta}-\theta\|_{{\rm L}^{1}_{loc}(\mu)}
and
\beta_{D}(\nu_{\delta},\nu)
. We use the notation
\|\cdot\|_{{\rm L}^{1}(D,\mu)}=\int_{D}|\cdot|\>d\mu
for the
{\rm L}^{1}
–norm in the open set
D
with respect to
\mu
.

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
0<a,b\leq 1
.
We assume that
S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) i.e.
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
. Let
0<\delta\leq\frac{R}{C}<1
(with
R=\min(R_{S,sg},R_{\theta,sg})<1
and
C=\max(C_{S,sg},C_{\theta,sg})\geq 1
) and
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open set, then

(117)

\beta_{D}(\nu_{\delta},\nu)\leq\|\theta_{\delta}-\theta\|_{{\rm L}^{1}(D,\mu)}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\theta_{\delta}-\theta\|_{{\rm L}^{1}(D,\mu)}\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b})\mu(D)+M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\delta^{d-l}\>\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2C\delta}\right)\>.

In the particular case where
D=B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an open ball of radius
0<\delta\leq R_{B}\leq\frac{R}{3C}
.
Then, recalling that
\xi_{l}=\xi_{l}(C\delta,B)
satisfies
\xi_{l}=1
if
B\cap(\mathfrak{S}_{l})^{C\delta}\neq\emptyset
and
\xi_{l}=0
otherwise,

(118)

\beta_{B}(\nu_{\delta},\nu)\leq\int_{B}\left|\theta_{\delta}-\theta\right|\>d\mu\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M\left(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b}\right)\mu(B)&\text{if }B\cap\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}=\emptyset\\
M\left(\displaystyle\delta^{a}+\delta^{b}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{\delta}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\right)\mu(B)&\text{in general,}\end{array}\right.

and for
0<r\leq R_{B}
and
x\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C(\delta+r)}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)\right\|\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b})
.

Proof.

Let
0<\delta\leq R
and
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open set. We first note that by definition of
\beta_{D}
,

\beta_{D}(\nu_{\delta},\nu)\leq\left|\nu_{\delta}-\nu\right|(D)=\int_{D}\left|\Phi(\theta_{\delta}(x))-\Phi(\theta(x))\right|\>d\mu(x)\leq M\int_{D}\left|\theta_{\delta}-\theta\right|\>d\mu\>.

Furthermore, we can consider separately
\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}
and
S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}
.
On one hand, applying (
113
) in Proposition
6.11
, we obtain

\int_{D\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}}\left|\theta_{\delta}-\theta\right|\>d\mu\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b})\mu(D)\>,

On the other hand, recalling (
60
): for
x\in S
,
|\theta_{\delta}(x)|\leq M
and using (
102
) with
C\delta\leq R

we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{D\cap\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}}\left|\theta_{\delta}-\theta\right|\>\underbrace{\>d\mu}_{\theta d\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}}

\displaystyle\leq M\mathcal{H}^{d}\left(\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}\cap D\cap S\right)\leq M\sum_{l=0}C^{d-l}\delta^{d-l}\>\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2C\delta}\right)\>,

hence concluding the proof of (
117
). The proof of (
118
) is exactly the same, but applying (
103
) instead of (
102
) in the last estimate above.
Finally, for
x\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C(\delta+r)}
, we have
B(x,r)\cap\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}=\emptyset
and
since
\|\psi_{r}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\varphi\|_{\infty}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{\varphi}r^{d}}\left\|\int_{B(x,r)}\psi_{r}\left(y-x\right)\>d\nu_{\delta}-\int_{B(x,r)}\psi_{r}\left(y-x\right)\>d\nu\right\|\leq\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\frac{1}{C_{\varphi}r^{d}}\left|\nu_{\delta}-\nu\right|(B(x,r))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{r^{d}}\int_{B(x,r)}|\theta_{\delta}-\theta|\>d\mu\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b})\frac{\mu(B(x,r))}{r^{d}}\quad\text{thanks to \eqref{eqThetaDeltaThetaL1}}

\displaystyle\leq MC_{0}\left(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b}\right)\>,

hence concluding the proof of Proposition
6.12
.
∎

We then similarly give uniform bounds for
\|\Sigma_{r}(\cdot,\nu_{\delta})-\Pi_{T_{\cdot}S}\|_{{\rm L}^{1}_{loc}(\mu)}
and
\|\sigma_{r,\delta}-\Pi_{T_{\cdot}S}\|_{{\rm L}^{1}_{loc}(\mu)}
as well as for
\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta},W_{S})
and
\beta_{B}(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta},W_{S})
.

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
0<a,b\leq 1
.
We assume that
S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) i.e.
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
. Let
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open ball of radius
0<R_{B}\leq\frac{R}{6C}
with
R=\min(R_{S,sg},R_{\theta,sg})<1
,
C=\max(C_{S,sg},C_{\theta,sg})\geq 1
and let
0<\delta,r<1
such that
\delta+r\leq R_{B}
.
Then,

(119)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle\int_{B}\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>d\mu(x),\quad\beta_{B}(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta},W_{S})&\leq&M\mu(B)\left(\displaystyle\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{a}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{\delta+r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\right)\\
\\
\displaystyle\int_{B}\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>d\mu(x),\quad\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta},W_{S})&\leq&M\mu(B)\left(\displaystyle\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{\min(a,b)}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{\delta+r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\right)\end{array}\>,

where
\xi_{l}=\xi_{l}(C(\delta+r),B)
satisfies
\xi_{l}=1
if
B\cap\mathfrak{S}^{C(\delta+r)}\neq\emptyset
and
\xi_{l}=0
otherwise.
We similarly have the following global estimates: let
0<\delta,r\leq R
with
\delta+r\leq\frac{R}{C}
and let
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open set, then

(120)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\int_{D}\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>d\mu(x),\>\beta_{D}(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta},W_{S})\leq M\mu(D^{r})\left(\displaystyle\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{a}\right)+M\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}(\delta+r)^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2C(\delta+r)}\right)\\
\\
\displaystyle\int_{D}\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta}-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>d\mu(x),\>\beta_{D}(W_{r,\delta},W_{S})\leq M\mu(D)\left(\displaystyle\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{\min(a,b)}\right)+\displaystyle M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\left(\delta+r\right)^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{2C(\delta+r)}\right)\end{array}

We recall that
W_{r,\delta}
and
\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta}
are defined in (
79
).

Remark 6.14

(Choice of tangent space estimator)
.

We observe that when considering only the deterministic part,

(1)

on one hand, first estimating the density to define
\nu_{\delta}=\frac{\theta}{\theta_{\delta}}\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\sim\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}
and then estimating the tangent plane thanks to
\Sigma_{r}(\cdot,\nu_{\delta})
(that is computing the covariance matrix with respect to the measure
\nu_{\delta}
whose density has been corrected) allows to obtain
r^{a}
in the r.h.s. of (
119
) ;

(2)

on the other hand, estimating the tangent space directly from
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}
(that is without correcting the density at this stage) through
\sigma_{r,\delta}
, we obtain instead
r^{\min(a,b)}
which involves the regularity of the density through
b
. In the case where the density is less regular than the tangent plane (in the sense
b<a
), the control is thus less accurate. However, this only impacts the global bound (
119
) if
r>\delta
.

Proof.

The proof builds upon estimates (
92
) and (
93
). We recall that
R\in(0,1)
and
C\geq 1
as in Definitions
6.2
and
6.5
.
First of all, as
r\leq R_{B}
,
\mu(B^{r})\leq\mu(2B)\leq M\mu(B)
by Ahlfors regularity of
\mu
.
Then, note that
\xi_{l}=\xi_{l}(C(\delta+r),B)
here in (
119
) and
\xi_{l}(C\delta,B^{r})\leq\xi_{l}(C\delta+r,B)\leq\xi_{l}(C(\delta+r),B)
. Indeed, if
x\in B^{r}\cap(\mathfrak{S}_{l})^{C\delta}\neq\emptyset
, then there exists
y\in B
and
z\in\mathfrak{S}_{l}
such that
|x-y|<r
and
|x-z|<C\delta
and then
|y-z|<r+C\delta
so that
y\in B\cap\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{C\delta+r}\subset B\cap\mathfrak{S}_{l}^{C(\delta+r)}\neq\emptyset
.
We can therefore apply (
118
) in Proposition
6.12
to the open ball
B^{r}
of radius
R_{B}+r
with
R_{B}\leq R_{B}+r\leq 2R_{B}\leq\frac{R}{3C}
and we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B^{r}}|\theta_{\delta}-\theta|\>d\mu

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}(C\delta,B^{r})\frac{\delta^{d-l}}{(R_{B}+r)^{d-l}}\right)\mu(B^{r})

(121)

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\underbrace{\xi_{l}(C(\delta+r),B)}_{\xi_{l}}\frac{\delta^{d-l}}{R_{B}^{d-l}}\right)\mu(B)\>.

We then partition
B=B_{1}\sqcup B_{2}
into
B_{1}=B\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C(\delta+r)}
and
B_{2}=B\cap\mathfrak{S}^{C(\delta+r)}
, so that we can use uniform bounds in
B_{1}
and control the measure of
B_{2}
(similarly to what is done in the proof of (
118
)). More precisely,
thanks to
Proposition
6.11
, using
\mu(B_{1})\leq\mu(B)
, we first have

\int_{B_{1}}\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu(x)\leq Mr^{a}\mu(B)\>.

Then, thanks to (
68
), for all
x\in S
,
\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\leq M+\|\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\leq M+1
so that using (
103
) (with
\delta+r\leq R_{B}
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2}}\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq M\mu(B_{2})\leq M\theta_{max}\mathcal{H}^{d}\left(B\cap S\cap\mathfrak{S}^{C(\delta+r)}\right)\leq M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\frac{(\delta+r)^{d-l}}{R_{B}^{d-l}}\mu(B)\>.

Consequently,

(122)

\int_{B}\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu(x)\leq M\left(r^{a}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\frac{(\delta+r)^{d-l}}{R_{B}^{d-l}}\right)\mu(B)\>,

and in view of (
93
), (
121
) and (
122
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{r}\beta_{B}\left(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta},W\right)\\
\displaystyle\int_{B}\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>d\mu(x)\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{B^{r}}|\theta_{\delta}-\theta|\>d\mu+M\int_{B}\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{a}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\frac{(\delta+r)^{d-l}}{R_{B}^{d-l}}\right)\mu(B)\>.

As
\max(\delta,r)\leq r+\delta
, we can use the same decomposition for
B
and thanks to (
114
) in Proposition
6.11
, we similarly have

\int_{B_{1}}\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\nu(x)\leq M(\delta^{a}+\delta^{b}+r^{a}+r^{b})\mu(B)\>,

while for
x\in S
,
\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)\|\leq\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu)\|\leq M
and

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2}}\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq(M+1)\mu(B_{2})\leq M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\frac{(\delta+r)^{d-l}}{R_{B}^{d-l}}\mu(B)\>,

hence leading, thanks to (
92
) and (
121
), to almost the same bound for
\displaystyle\int_{B}\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>d\mu(x)
and
\beta_{B}\left(W_{r,\delta},W_{S}\right)
as for
\beta_{B}\left(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta},W_{S}\right)
(up to replacing
r^{a}
with
r^{\min(a,b)}
), which conclude the proof of (
119
).

The proof of (
120
) is then a straightforward adaptation, replacing the use of the local estimates (
118
) in Proposition
6.12
and (
103
) with the global ones (
117
) in Proposition
6.12
and (
102
).
∎

6.4.
Convergence rate of the varifold estimator in a piecewise Hölder regularity class

In Section
6.4
, we can eventually address the inference of the varifold structure. Up to this point, we proposed convergent estimators
W_{r,\delta,N}
,
\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N}
of
W_{r,\delta}
,
\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta}
(see Proposition
5.8
) that can be coupled to the accuracy of the regularization established in Proposition
6.13
to give estimators of
W_{S}=\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\otimes\delta_{\Pi_{T_{x}S}}
(see (
129
)). However, such estimators
W_{r,\delta,N}
,
\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N}
(with
\delta=\delta_{N}
,
r=r_{N}
) are Radon measures in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)
that are not
d
–varifolds since
\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(x)
or
\Sigma_{r}(\cdot,\nu_{\delta,N})
are not orthogonal projectors in general. The last step to obtain an estimator
V_{r,\delta,N}
(resp.
\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N}
) in the space of varifolds is to replace the symmetric matrix
\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(x)
(resp.
\Sigma_{r}(\cdot,\nu_{\delta,N})
) with an orthogonal projection of rank
d
that will be denoted by
\pi_{r,\delta,N}(x)
(resp.
\Pi_{r}(\cdot,\nu_{\delta,N})
).
We then consider
V_{r,\delta,N}=\nu_{\delta,N}\otimes\delta_{\pi_{r,\delta,N}}
and
\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N}=\nu_{\delta,N}\otimes\delta_{\Pi_{r}(\cdot,\nu_{\delta,N})}
that are Radon measures in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)
with support in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times{\rm P}_{d,n}
and can be identified with
d
–varifolds through the bi-Lipschitz correspondence
\mathcal{I}
of Proposition
2.17
. We recall the notation
{\rm P}_{d,n}\subset{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)
for the set of orthogonal projectors of rank
d
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Beyond the formal difference between Radon measures in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)

W_{r,\delta,N}
,
\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N}
and
d
–varifolds
V_{r,\delta,N}
,
\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N}
, it is important to have such a varifold estimator if we keep in mind the idea of estimating curvature quantities relying on the deterministic approximations proposed in
[
BLM17
]
and
[
BLM22
]
in the future. We now explain how we pass from a symmetric matrix
to a rank
d
orthogonal projector via the eigen decomposition, note that this construction is consistent with what is often performed numerically, including in both aforementioned works
[
BLM17
]
and
[
BLM22
]
.

Given
\Sigma\in{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)
, we associate a rank–
d
orthogonal projector
\Pi\in{\rm P}_{d,n}
as follows: let
\lambda_{1}(\Sigma)\geq\lambda_{2}(\Sigma)\geq\ldots\geq\lambda_{n}(\Sigma)
be the ordered eigenvalues of
\Sigma
, associated with an orthonormal basis of eigenvectors
(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
, we then define
\Pi=\sum_{k=1}^{d}u_{k}\otimes u_{k}
.
Note that such a matrix
\Pi
depends on the choice of the orthonormal basis
(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
in the case where
\lambda_{d}(\Sigma)=\lambda_{d+1}(\Sigma)
. In what follows, we do not exclude such a possibility and we consider that
\Pi
can be taken to be any of the admissible choice. Then, for any projector
\tilde{\Pi}\in{\rm P}_{d,n}
, we have

(123)

\|\Sigma-\Pi\|\leq\|\Sigma-\tilde{\Pi}\|\>.

Indeed, as
\Sigma=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{k}(\Sigma)u_{k}\otimes u_{k}
and by definition of
\Pi
, the symmetric matrix
\Sigma-\Pi=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\lambda_{k}(\Sigma)-\varepsilon_{k}\right)u_{k}\otimes u_{k}
has eigenvalues
\lambda_{k}(\Sigma)-\varepsilon_{k}
with the notation
\varepsilon_{k}\in\{0,1\}
,
\varepsilon_{k}=1
if
k\leq d
and
\varepsilon_{k}=0
if
k\geq d+1
. We therefore obtain on one hand

(124)

\|\Sigma-\Pi\|=\max_{k=1\ldots n}\left|\lambda_{k}(\Sigma)-\varepsilon_{k}\right|\>.

On the other hand, given
\tilde{\Pi}\in{\rm P}_{d,n}
, we observe that
\Pi
and
\tilde{\Pi}
have same eigenvalues
(\varepsilon_{k})_{k=1\ldots n}
and therefore, applying Weyl inequality (more precisely, the fact that the ordered eigenvalues are
1
–Lipschitz among real symmetric or hermitian matrices endowed with the operator norm), we obtain for
k=1,\ldots,n
,

\displaystyle\left|\lambda_{k}\left(\Sigma\right)-\varepsilon_{k}\right|

\displaystyle=|\lambda_{k}\left(\Sigma\right)-\lambda_{k}(\tilde{\Pi})|\leq\|\Sigma-\tilde{\Pi}\|

and from (
124
) we thus infer (
123
).
In the particular case where
\Sigma
is of the form
\Sigma_{r}(x,\lambda)
(see Definition
5.2
) for
r>0
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\lambda
a finite Radon measure in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we use the notation
\Pi_{r}(x,\lambda)
for the associated orthogonal projector of rank
d
and we obtain with
\tilde{\Pi}=\Pi_{T_{x}S}
in (
123
):

(125)

\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})-\Pi_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right\|\leq\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>,

while in the case where
\Sigma
is of the form
\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(x)
, we use the notation
\pi_{r,\delta,N}(x)
for the associated orthogonal projector of rank
d
and we similarly obtain

(126)

\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(x)-\pi_{r,\delta,N}(x)\right\|\leq\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\>,

Building upon such controls (
125
) and (
126
), we introduce the
d
–varifolds

(127)

V_{r,\delta,N}=\nu_{\delta,N}\otimes\delta_{\pi_{r,\delta,N}(x)}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N}=\nu_{\delta,N}\otimes\delta_{\Pi_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})}

and we investigate the convergence of
V_{r,\delta,N}
, (respectively
\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N}
) towards
W_{S}=\nu\otimes\delta_{\Pi_{T_{x}S}}
. Combining the mean convergence rate obtained in Proposition
5.8
for
\mathbb{E}[\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta,N},\>W_{r,\delta})]
(respectively
\mathbb{E}[\beta_{B}(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N},\>\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta})]
) with the uniform bound obtained for
\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta},\>W_{S})
(respectively
\beta_{B}(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta},\>W_{S})
) in Proposition
6.13
we thus obtain a convergent varifold estimator of
W_{S}
with explicit mean convergence rate in the regularity class
\mathcal{P}
at hand, as stated in Theorem
6.15
. Note that we only state a local version in balls, for the sake of clarity, it is however possible to directly write a global estimate, which will be done in Theorem
7.4
with refined convergence rate.

Theorem 6.15
.

Let
0<a,b\leq 1
.
We assume that
S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) i.e.
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
. Let
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open ball of radius
0<R_{B}\leq\frac{R}{6C}
with
R=\min(R_{S,sg},R_{\theta,sg})<1
,
C=\max(C_{S,sg},C_{\theta,sg})\geq 1
and let
0<\delta,r<1
with
\delta+r\leq R_{B}
.
Then, for
N\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
large enough so that
N^{-\frac{1}{d}}\leq\min(\delta,r)
, we have

(128)

\left.\begin{array}[]{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N},\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N})\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N},W_{S})\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N},W_{S})\right]\end{array}\right|\leq M\mu(B)\left[\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{a}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{\delta+r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\hskip-3.0pt+\frac{1}{\min(\delta,r)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right.\right]

and

(129)

\left.\begin{array}[]{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta,N},V_{r,\delta,N})\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta,N},W_{S})\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(V_{r,\delta,N},W_{S})\right]\end{array}\right|\leq M\mu(B)\left[\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{\min(a,b)}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{\delta+r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\hskip-15.0pt+\frac{1}{\min(\delta,r)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right.\right]

where
\xi_{l}=\xi_{l}(C(\delta+r),B)
satisfies
\xi_{l}=1
if
B\cap\mathfrak{S}^{C(\delta+r)}\neq\emptyset
and
\xi_{l}=0
otherwise.

If the ball
B
is allowed to have larger radius
0<R_{B}<1
(instead of
R_{B}\leq R
), the same statements hold for
\delta+r\leq\frac{R}{C}
and replacing in both right hand sides in (
128
) and (
129
):

(130)

\mu(B)\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{\delta+r}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\quad\text{with}\quad\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}(\delta+r)^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap B^{2C(\delta+r)})\>.

Proof.

We recall that
M
is a generic constant (see Remark
6.6
). We start with proving the theorem concerning
\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N}
and
\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N}
.
Let
f\in{\rm C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n))
with
\supp f\subset B\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)
,
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
{\rm Lip}(f)\leq 1
. Note that
\supp f\subset B\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)
so that
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq R_{B}
. Then, thanks to (
125
), we have for
x\in S
,

\displaystyle\left|f\left(x,\Pi_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right)-f\left(x,\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Pi_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right\|\leq\left\|\Pi_{T_{x}S}-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Pi_{T_{x}S}-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})\right\|+\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right\|

and consequently, as
\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\leq M
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int f\>d\widetilde{V}_{r,\delta,N}-\int f\>d\widetilde{W}_{r,\delta,N}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int\left[f\left(x,\Pi_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right)-f\left(x,\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\right)\right]\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}(x)\right)\>d\mu_{N}\right|

(131)

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}+M\int_{B}\|\Pi_{T_{x}S}-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})\|\>d\mu_{N}

with
\displaystyle g(x)=\min\left(2R_{B},\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})\|\right)
.
On one hand, we know from Proposition
6.13
that

(132)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B}\|\Pi_{T_{x}S}-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})\|\>d\mu_{N}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{B}\|\Pi_{T_{x}S}-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})\|\>d\mu\leq M\left(\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{a}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{r+\delta}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\right)\mu(B)\>.

On the other hand, let us check that
g
satisfies the assumption (
36
) of Proposition
4.3
with
\displaystyle\kappa_{1}=0
,
\displaystyle\kappa_{2}=M
,
\displaystyle\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(r)=\frac{M}{r}
and
\kappa=R_{B}
.
Indeed, for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, using that
\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\leq M
,
{\rm Lip}(\psi_{r})\leq\frac{M}{r}
(see (
67
)) and the Ahlfors regularity of
\mu
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Sigma_{r}(y,\nu_{\delta})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{\varphi}r^{d}}\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\|\psi_{r}(z-x)-\psi_{r}(z-y)\right\|\left|\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta}(z)\right)\right|\>d\mu(z)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{r^{d+1}}\mu\left(B(x,r)\cup B(y,r)\right)|x-y|\leq\frac{M}{r}|x-y|\>,

and similarly, see also (
89
),

\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})-\Sigma_{r}(y,\nu_{\delta,N})\right\|\leq\frac{M}{r^{d+1}}\mu_{N}\left(B(x,r)\cup B(y,r)\right)|x-y|=\frac{M}{r}\Delta_{r,N}(x,y)|x-y|\>,

so that by triangular inequality, and noting that
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\mapsto\min(2R_{B},t)
is
1
–Lipschitz, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|g(x)-g(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})-\Sigma_{r}(y,\nu_{\delta,N})\|+\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})-\Sigma_{r}(y,\nu_{\delta})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{r}\Delta_{r,N}(x,y)|x-y|+\frac{M}{r}|x-y|\>.

Moreover
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2R_{B}
so that
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq\kappa\kappa_{0}
with
\kappa\leq R_{B}
.
We can thus apply Proposition
4.3
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}-\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]\leq\frac{M}{r}\mu(B)\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right.

and we recall that thanks to (
74
) in Proposition
5.6
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]\leq\int_{B}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})\|\right]\>d\mu(x)\leq\frac{M}{\delta}\mu(B)\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right.

so that we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}-\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{1}{r}\right)\mu(B)\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right..

We can conclude the proof of (
128
) (first line) thanks to (
131
), (
132
) and (
6.4
). We then obtain (
128
) as a direct consequence of
Proposition
5.8
(ii)
and Proposition
6.13
.

Let us check that the same strategy allows to prove Theorem
6.15
for
W_{r,\delta,N}
and
W_{r,\delta,N}
. Similarly to (
131
), we have

(136)

\displaystyle\left|\int f\>dV_{r,\delta,N}-\int f\>dW_{r,\delta,N}\right|\leq M\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}+M\int_{B}\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu_{N}

with
\displaystyle g(x)=\min\left(2R_{B},\|\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(x)-\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)\|\right)
.
Again from Proposition
6.13
, we know that

(137)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B}\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu_{N}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{B}\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>d\mu\leq M\left[\delta^{\min(a,b)}+r^{\min(a,b)}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\left(\frac{r+\delta}{R_{B}}\right)^{d-l}\right]\mu(B)\>.

We similarly check that
g
satisfies the assumption (
36
) of Proposition
4.3
with
\displaystyle\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=\kappa_{3}=M
,
\displaystyle\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(\delta,r)=\frac{M}{\delta}+\frac{M}{r}
and
\kappa=R_{B}
. Indeed, thanks to (
67
), (
68
)
for
x
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta}(x)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sigma_{r,\delta}(y)\right\|=\left\|\Phi(\theta_{\delta}(x))\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu)-\Phi(\theta_{\delta}(y))\Sigma_{r}(y,\mu)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu)-\Sigma_{r}(y,\mu)\|+\|\Sigma_{r}(y,\mu)\|{\rm Lip}(\Phi)|\theta_{\delta}(x)-\theta_{\delta}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{C_{\varphi}r^{d}}\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\|\psi_{r}(z-x)-\psi_{r}(z-y)\right\|\>d\mu(z)+\frac{M}{C_{\eta}\delta^{d}}\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\eta_{\delta}(z-x)-\eta_{\delta}(z-y)\right|\>d\mu(z)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{r^{d+1}}\mu\left(B(x,r)\cup B(y,r)\right)|x-y|+\frac{M}{\delta^{d+1}}\mu\left(B(x,\delta)\cup B(y,\delta)\right)|x-y|

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{1}{r}\right)|x-y|\text{ by Ahlfors regularity}\>.

And similarly, see also (
86
),

\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(x)-\sigma_{r,\delta,N}(y)\right\|\leq M\left(\frac{1}{r}\Delta_{r,N}(x,y)+\frac{1}{\delta}\Delta_{\delta,N}(x,y)\Delta_{r,N}(x,y)\right)|x-y|\>\>,

so that by triangular inequality,

\displaystyle\left|g(x)-g(y)\right|\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{\delta}\Delta_{\delta,N}(x,y)+\frac{1}{r}\Delta_{r,N}(x,y)+\frac{1}{\delta}\Delta_{\delta,N}(x,y)\Delta_{r,N}(x,y)\right)|x-y|\>.

We can thus apply Proposition
4.3
in order to control
\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}-\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]
and we recall that thanks to (
73
) in Proposition
5.6
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]\leq\int_{B}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta,N})-\Sigma_{r}(x,\nu_{\delta})\|\right]\>d\mu(x)\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nr^{d}}}\right)\mu(B)

so that we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu_{N}-\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{B}g\>d\mu\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{1}{r}\right)\mu(B)\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right.+M\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nr^{d}}}\right)\mu(B)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{\min(\delta,r)}\mu(B)\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right.\>.

We can conclude the proof of (
129
) (first line) thanks to (
136
), (
137
) and (
6.4
). We then obtain (
129
) as a direct consequence of Proposition
5.8
and Proposition
6.13
.

The proof of (
130
) is exactly the same, replacing the local estimate (
119
) with the global one (
120
) in each application of Proposition
6.13
(also noting that
B^{r}\subset 2B
and thus
\mu(B^{r})\leq M\mu(B)
) when establishing (
132
) and (
137
).
∎

7.
Inferring the varifold structure in a piecewise Hölder regularity class

In Section
6.4
and more precisely in Theorem
6.15
, we quantify the speed rate convergence of the deterministic term, that is the Bounded Lipschitz distance
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta},W_{S})\right]
thanks to the piecewise Hölder regularity assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) i.e. for
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
.
However, we did not take full advantage of such regularity: we directly apply Proposition
5.8
that gives the following bound, only relies on
(H_{1})
and
(H_{2})

\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta,N},W_{r,\delta})\right]\leq\frac{M}{\min(\delta,r)}\mu(B)\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}N^{-1/d}&\text{if}&d>2\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N&\text{if}&d=2\\
N^{-1/2}&\text{if}&d=1\end{array}\right.\>.

The purpose of the current section is precisely to improve the control of
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta,N},W_{r,\delta})\right]
in the piecewise Hölder regularity class
\mathcal{P}
defined in Section
6.1
. Note that we will rely on the fact that when considering
\sigma_{r,\delta,N}
the concentration result (
73
) gives a better rate than (
74
) obtained for
\Sigma_{r}(\cdot,\nu_{\delta})
: see Remark
5.7
. We therefore carry on our study considering only the tangent space estimator
\sigma_{r,\delta,N}
.

To this end, we need to estimate independently the measure
\mu
, the density
\theta
and the tangent plane
T_{x}S
: we can divide our sample into
4
independent parts,
(X_{j})_{4j+k}
for
k=0,1,2,3
, for instance. For simplicity, we assume that the sample has size
4N
and we use the notations
{\bf X}=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{N})
,
{\bf Y}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{N})
,

{\tilde{\bf Y}}=(\tilde{Y}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{Y}_{N})

and
{\bf Z}=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N})
for the four independent subsamples. We consequently have four associated empirical measures
\mu_{N}^{\bf X}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{X_{i}}
and similarly
\mu_{N}^{\bf Y}
,
\mu_{N}^{\tilde{\bf Y}}
and
\mu_{N}^{\bf Z}
. We can then independently estimate the measure
\mu
, the density
\theta
and the tangent plane
T_{x}S
by considering for
\delta,r>0
:

\mu_{N}^{\bf X}\>,\quad\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}=\Theta_{\delta}(\cdot,\mu_{N}^{\bf Y})=\frac{\mu_{N}^{\bf Y}\ast\eta_{\delta}}{C_{\eta}\delta^{d}}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma_{r,\delta,N}=\sigma_{r,\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}=\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y}}\right)\Sigma_{r}(\cdot,\mu_{N}^{\bf Z})\>.

In Lemma
7.3
, we evidence that in the piecewise Hölder regularity class
\mathcal{P}
at hand,
\theta_{\delta,N}
and
\sigma_{r,\delta,N}
admit mean Lipschitz estimates (
145
) and (
147
) finer than the generic rough estimates previously used: for instance concerning
\theta_{\delta,N}
, we had in Lemma
4.7
that for
x
,
y\in S
,

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{\delta,N}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}(y)\right|\leq\frac{M}{\delta}\Delta_{\delta,N}(x,y)|x-y|
and
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{\delta,N}(x,y)\right]\leq M

so that we were using the mean Lipschitz estimate
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}(y)\right|\right]\leq\frac{M}{\delta}|x-y|
in the proof of Theorem
4.9
and Proposition
5.8
. Such mean Lipschitz constants of order
\delta^{-1}
for
\theta_{\delta,N}
and
\min(\delta,r)^{-1}
for
\sigma_{r,\delta,N}
are responsible for the same factor appearing in the rates established in those results (Theorem
4.9
and Proposition
5.8
). Therefore, a careful adaptation of the proof of Proposition
4.3
allows to leverage the aforementioned improved Lipschitz estimates (
145
) and (
147
) in the piecewise Hölder regularity class
\mathcal{P}
to infer Theorem
7.4
: we eventually obtain a varifold estimator
\widehat{V}_{N}=\widehat{V}_{\delta_{N},N}
(defined in (
149
)) with a mean convergence rate of order

\mathbb{E}\left[\beta(\widehat{V}_{N},W_{S})\right]\lesssim N^{\displaystyle-\frac{c}{d+2c}}\quad\text{with}\quad c=\min(a,b)\quad\text{choosing }\delta_{N}=N^{\displaystyle-\frac{1}{d+2c}}\>.

Remark 7.1
.

Note that
S
being compact, it is sufficient to state Theorem
7.4
in bounded open sets
D
. Indeed, we know that
S\subset B(0,k_{0})
for some
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and then it is not difficult to check that
\beta(\cdot,\cdot)\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\beta_{B(0,k_{0}+k)}
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
, thanks to a
\frac{1}{k}
–Lipschitz radial cutoff equal to
1
in
B(0,k_{0})
and
0
outside
B(0,k_{0}+k)
.

Remark 7.2

(Constant
M
)
.

All along the current section,
M
stands for a generic constant that may vary from one statement to another one, and only depends on
d,n,\widetilde{C_{0}},\eta,\varphi,\theta_{min/max},C=\max(C_{\theta,sg},C_{S,sg}),R=\min(R_{\theta,sg},R_{S,sg})
and
\tau
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}

and
0<a,b\leq 1
.
We assume that

S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) i.e.
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
. Using the notations of Definitions
6.2
and
6.5
, let
0<\delta,r\leq R=\min(R_{S,sg},R_{\theta,sg})<1
and
\rho=\max(\delta,r)
,
then,
for all
x,y\in S\setminus\left(\mathfrak{S}^{C\rho}\right)
with
|x-y|\leq\rho
, we have

(144)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta(x)\right|\right]\leq M\left(\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)

and

(145)

\mathbb{E}\left[|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(y)|\right]\leq M\left(|x-y|^{b}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)\>,

as well as

(146)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\right]\leq M\left(r^{\min(a,b)}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nr^{d}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)

and

(147)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}(x)-\sigma_{r,\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}(y)\right\|\right]\leq M\left(|x-y|^{a}+r^{\min(a,b)}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nr^{d}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)\>.

Proof.

The proof is a straightforward combination of previously stated results, we recall that
\mathfrak{S}=S_{sg}\cup\Theta_{sg}
.
Let
x,y\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C\rho}\subset S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C\delta}
with
|x-y|\leq\rho
.
Applying Proposition
3.5
and Proposition
6.11
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta(x)\right|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta}(x)\right|\right]+\left|\theta_{\delta}(x)-\theta(x)\right|\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}\right)\>.

The same estimate holds at point
y
and thus,
triangular inequality and (

H
7

) conclude the proof of (
145
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(y)|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta(x)\right|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(y)-\theta(y)\right|\right]+\left|\theta(x)-\theta(y)\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2M\left(\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)+C|x-y|^{b}\>.

Thanks to Proposition
5.6
and Proposition
6.11
, we similarly have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu_{N}^{\bf Z})-\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\right]\leq M\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nr^{d}}}+r^{\min(a,b)}\right)\>.

Then, recalling that
\Pi_{T_{x}S}=\Phi(\theta(x))\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sigma_{r,\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y}}(x)\right)\Sigma_{r}(x,\mu_{N}^{\bf Z})-\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y}}(x)\right)\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y}}(x)\right)\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}-\Phi(\theta(x))\theta(x)\Pi_{T_{x}S}\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\Phi\|_{\infty}M\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nr^{d}}}+r^{\min(a,b)}\right)+\theta_{max}{\rm Lip}(\Phi)\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf\tilde{Y}}(x)-\theta(x)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nr^{d}}}+r^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}\right)\quad\text{thanks to \eqref{eqConcentrationThetadeltaN}, }{\bf\tilde{Y}},{\bf Y}\text{ having same law,}

hence proving (
146
).
It remains to combine (
146
) at
x
and
y
with
(

H
5

):
\displaystyle\left\|\Pi_{T_{x}S}-\Pi_{T_{y}S}\right\|\leq C|x-y|^{a}
, to obtain (
147
) and conclude the proof of Lemma
7.3
.
∎

We observe that when adding up bounds (
145
) and (
147
),
\delta
and
r
play symmetric roles, that is also true concerning the control we obtained in Proposition
6.13
(see the second estimate) for the deterministic term
\beta_{B}(W_{r,\delta},W_{S})
and we consequently fix
\delta=r
hereafter. We thus eventually consider

(148)

\widehat{\nu}_{\delta,N}=\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}\right)\mu_{N}^{\bf X}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{W}_{\delta,N}=\widehat{\nu}_{\delta,N}\otimes\delta_{\sigma_{\delta,N}}\quad\text{with}\quad\sigma_{\delta,N}=\sigma_{\delta,\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}=\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y}}\right)\Sigma_{\delta}(\cdot,\mu_{N}^{\bf Z})\>.

We also define the varifold estimator

(149)

\widehat{V}_{\delta,N}=\widehat{\nu}_{\delta,N}\otimes\delta_{\pi_{\delta,N}}\>,

where we recall that
\pi_{\delta,N}=\pi_{\delta,r,N}
for
\delta=r
is the truncation of the symmetric matrix
\sigma_{\delta,N}
according to its
d
highest eigenvalues as explained in Section
6.4
and we recall (
126
):
for
x\in S
,

(150)

\|\sigma_{\delta,N}(x)-\pi_{\delta,N}(x)\|\leq\|\sigma_{\delta,N}(x)-\Pi_{T_{x}S}\|\>.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
0<a,b\leq 1
.
We assume that

S
and
\theta
satisfy assumptions
(H_{1})
to (

H
7

) i.e.
\mu=\theta\mathcal{H}^{d}_{|S}\in\mathcal{P}
. Let
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a bounded open set. We recall that
R=\min(R_{S,sg},R_{\theta,sg})<1
and
let
N\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
be large enough so that
\delta_{N}:=N^{\displaystyle-\frac{1}{d+2\min(a,b)}}\leq\frac{R}{20C}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{D}\left(\widehat{\nu}_{\delta_{N},N},\nu\right)\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{D}(\widehat{W}_{\delta_{N},N},W_{S})\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{D}(\widehat{V}_{\delta_{N},N},W_{S})\right]\end{array}\right\}\leq M\mu(D^{\delta_{N}})\delta_{N}^{\min(a,b)}+M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\delta_{N}^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{20C\delta_{N}}\right)\leq\widetilde{M}\>\delta_{N}^{\min(a,b)}\>,

with
\widetilde{M}=M\left(\mu(D^{1})+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\mathcal{H}^{l}\left(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{1}\right)\right)\leq M\left(1+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l})\right)
.

In the particular case where
D=B
is a ball of radius
R_{B}\leq\frac{R}{3}
, we have the local estimate

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}\left(\widehat{\nu}_{\delta_{N},N},\nu\right)\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(\widehat{W}_{\delta_{N},N},W_{S})\right]\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{B}(\widehat{V}_{\delta_{N},N},W_{S})\right]\end{array}\right\}\leq M\mu(B)\left(\delta_{N}^{\min(a,b)}+\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\xi_{l}\frac{\delta_{N}^{d-l}}{R_{B}^{d-l}}\right)\>,

where
\xi_{l}=\xi_{l}(20C\delta_{N},B)
satisfies
\xi_{l}=1
if
B\cap\mathfrak{S}^{20C\delta_{N}}\neq\emptyset
and
\xi_{l}=0
otherwise.

Proof of Theorem
7.4
.

Let
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a bounded open set.
We fix
N\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
,
0<\delta\leq\frac{R}{20C}
satisfying
N^{-\frac{1}{d}}\leq\delta
(and we recall that we fixed
r=\delta
in (
148
)).
In view of proving (
7.4
), we will consider two types of test functions:

(1)

either
f\in{\rm C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R})
is a
1
–Lipschitz function such that
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and

\supp f\subset D
, and in this case we introduce
g_{\delta,N}(x)=g_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)=f(x)\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)\right)
and
g_{\delta}(x)=f(x)\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta}(x)\right)
;

(2)

or
f\in{\rm C}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n),\mathbb{R})
is a
1
–Lipschitz function such that
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and

\supp f\subset D\times{\rm Sym}_{+}(n)
, and in this case we introduce
g_{\delta,N}(x)=g_{\delta,N}^{{\bf Y},\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}(x)=f\left(x,\sigma_{\delta,N}^{\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}}(x)\right)\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)\right)
and
g_{\delta}(x)=f\left(x,\sigma_{\delta,\delta}(x)\right)\Phi\left(\theta_{\delta}(x)\right)
.

In both cases,

g_{\delta,N}
and
\mu_{N}^{\bf X}
are independent, we will shorten
(i)
or
(ii)
with
f\in{\rm BL}
so that

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{r}\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{D}\left(\widehat{\nu}_{\delta,N},\nu_{\delta}\right)\right]\text{ in case }(i)\\
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\beta_{D}\left(\widehat{W}_{\delta,N},W_{\delta,\delta}\right)\right]\text{ in case }(ii)\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}\left|\int_{D}g_{\delta,N}\>d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-\int_{D}g_{\delta}\>d\mu\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}\left|\int_{D}g_{\delta,N}\>\left(d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-d\mu\right)\right|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}\left|\int_{D}\left(g_{\delta,N}-g_{\delta}\right)\>d\mu\right|\right]

(159)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}\left|\int_{D}g_{\delta,N}\>\left(d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-d\mu\right)\right|\right]+\frac{M}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\mu(D)\>,

where the last estimate follows from Proposition
3.5
and Proposition
5.6
since

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}\int_{D}\left|g_{\delta,N}-g_{\delta}\right|\>d\mu\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\int_{D}\|\sigma_{\delta,N}-\sigma_{\delta,\delta}\|\>d\mu+{\rm Lip}(\Phi)\int_{D}\left|\theta_{\delta,N}-\theta_{\delta}\right|\>d\mu\right]\leq\frac{M}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\mu(D)\>.

We are left with estimating
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}\left|\int_{D}g_{\delta,N}\>\left(d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-d\mu\right)\right|\right]
, which is the purpose of Steps
1
and
2
. As we explained in the introduction of Section
7
, we want to leverage the mean Lipschitz estimates (
145
) and (
147
) that are finer than the worst case Lipschitz estimate in the regularity class
\mathcal{P}
, which prevents from applying Proposition
4.3
. Instead, we follow and adapt the steps of the proof of Proposition
4.3
hereafter.

We use the notations
\varepsilon=N^{-\frac{1}{d}}\leq\delta
,
\varepsilon_{u}:=3^{-u}
for
u\in\mathbb{N}
, and
T:=D\cap S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{10C\delta}
.

We define integers
0\leq s\leq t
such that

3^{-(t+1)}<\varepsilon\leq 3^{-t}=\varepsilon_{t}\quad\text{and}\quad 3^{-(s+1)}<\varepsilon^{\alpha}\leq 3^{-s}=\varepsilon_{s}\quad\text{and}\quad\varepsilon_{s}\leq\delta\>,

where
0<\alpha\leq 1
is defined hereafter depending on the case
(i)
or
(ii)
.
We recall that
T
can be partitioned with
m_{u}
pieces of diameter
\leq\varepsilon_{u}
and
m_{u}
furthermore satisfies the estimate (
40
)

m_{u}\leq 4^{d}C_{0}\varepsilon_{u}^{-d}\mu\left(D^{\frac{\varepsilon_{u}}{4}}\right)\>.

We can apply Lemma
4.2
to such partitions and define nested partitions
\left\{A_{j}^{u}\>:\>u=s,\ldots,t,\,j=1,\ldots,m_{u}\right\}
satisfying (
31
) and (
32
).
For each
u=s,\dots,t
and
j=1,\dots,m_{u}
we choose
x^{u}_{j}\in A_{j}^{u}
and
we introduce

M_{u}:=\sum_{j=1}^{m_{u}}|\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(A^{u}_{j})-\mu(A^{u}_{j})|\quad\text{and}\quad I_{u}:=\left|\sum_{j=1}^{m_{u}}g_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{u})(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(A^{u}_{j})-\mu(A^{u}_{j}))\right|\>.

Step
1
:
we can prove the following control:

(160)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}\left|\int_{D}g_{\delta,N}\>\left(d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-d\mu\right)\right|\right]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}I_{t}\right]+M\left(\varepsilon^{\min(a,b)}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)\mu(D)+M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\delta^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{20C\delta})\>.

Indeed, we remind (see (
31
)) that
(A_{j}^{t})_{j=1}^{m_{t}}
is a partition of
T=D\cap S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{10C\delta}
and for all
j
,
{\rm diam}A_{j}^{t}\leq 3\varepsilon_{t}\leq 9\varepsilon
. As
\|g_{\delta,N}\|_{\infty}\leq\|f\|_{\infty}\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\leq M
, we first have

(161)

\Bigg|\int_{D}g_{\delta,N}\>\left(d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-d\mu\right)\Bigg|\leq M\left(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}\ \left(D\cap\mathfrak{S}^{10C\delta}\right)+\mu\left(D\cap\mathfrak{S}^{10C\delta}\right)\right)+\left|\int_{T}g_{\delta,N}\>\left(d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-d\mu\right)\right|\>.

The first term in the right hand side of (
161
) is now independent of
f
and when taking the mean value, we have by (
102
) (with
\rho=10C\delta\leq R
):

(162)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mu_{N}^{\bf X}\left(D\cap\mathfrak{S}^{10C\delta}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mu\left(D\cap\mathfrak{S}^{10C\delta}\right)\leq\theta_{max}\mathcal{H}^{d}\left(D\cap\mathfrak{S}^{10C\delta}\cap S\right)\leq M\sum_{l=0}^{d-1}\delta^{d-l}\mathcal{H}^{l}(\mathfrak{S}_{l}\cap D^{20C\delta})\>.

Moreover, regarding the last term in the right hand side of (
161
), we have for
x\in A_{j}^{t}
,
|x-x_{j}^{t}|\leq{\rm diam}A_{j}^{t}\leq 9\varepsilon
and thus, in case
(i)
or
(ii)
:

\displaystyle\left|g_{\delta,N}(x)-g_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{\infty}{\rm Lip}(\Phi)\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x_{j}^{t})\right|+\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\left|f(x)-f(x_{j}^{t})\right|&(i)\\
\left|f\left(x,\sigma_{\delta,N}(x)\right)-f\left(x_{j}^{t},\sigma_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{t})\right)\right|&(ii)\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\leq M\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x_{j}^{t})\right|+M\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}9\varepsilon&(i)\\
9\varepsilon+\left\|\sigma_{\delta,N}(x)-\sigma_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{t})\right\|&(ii)\end{array}\right.\>.

Observing that for
\delta=r
, the right hand side of (
145
) and (
147
) are similar and bounded by

M\left(|x-x_{j}^{t}|^{\min(a,b)}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)\leq M\left((9\varepsilon)^{\min(a,b)}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)\>,

we can handle
(i)
and
(ii)
in the same way and we obtain thanks to (
7
):

(167)

\left|\int_{T}g_{\delta,N}\left(d\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-d\mu\right)\right|=\left|\sum^{m_{t}}_{j=1}\int_{A_{j}^{t}}g_{\delta,N}(x)\>d(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-\mu)(x)\right|\leq I_{t}+\left|\sum^{m_{t}}_{j=1}\int_{A_{j}^{t}}\left(g_{\delta,N}(x)-g_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{t})\right)\>d(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}-\mu)(x)\right|\\
\leq I_{t}+M\varepsilon\left(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(D)+\mu(D)\right)+M\sum^{m_{t}}_{j=1}\int\limits_{A_{j}^{t}}\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x_{j}^{t})\right|+\left\|\sigma_{\delta,N}(x)-\sigma_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{t})\right\|\>d(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}+\mu)(x)\>.

By independence of
{\bf X}
and
{\bf Y}
and Lemma
7.3
(noting that
x,x_{j}^{t}\in S\setminus\mathfrak{S}^{C10\delta}
and
|x-x_{j}^{t}|\leq 9\varepsilon\leq 10\delta
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum^{m_{t}}_{j=1}\int\limits_{A_{j}^{t}}\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x_{j}^{t})\right|\>d(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}+\mu)(x)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}\left[\sum^{m_{t}}_{j=1}\int\limits_{A_{j}^{t}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf Y}\left[\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x)-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x_{j}^{t})\right|\right]\>d(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}+\mu)(x)\right]

(168)

\displaystyle\leq 2M\left(\varepsilon^{\min(a,b)}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)\mu(D)\>,

and similarly

(169)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum^{m_{t}}_{j=1}\int\limits_{A_{j}^{t}}\left\|\sigma_{\delta,N}(x)-\sigma_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{t})\right\|\>d(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}+\mu)(x)\right]\leq M\left(\varepsilon^{\min(a,b)}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\delta^{d}}}\right)\mu(D)\>.

Combining (
161
), (
162
), (
167
), (
168
), (
169
) with
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon^{\min(a,b)}
we finally infer (
160
) and conclude Step
1
.

Step
2
:
We now estimate
I_{t}
by induction on
u
from
t
to
s
:

(170)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}I_{t}\right]\leq M\mu\left(D^{\delta}\right)\left(\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)\frac{d}{2}}+\delta^{\min(a,b)}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\delta^{\frac{d}{2}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{u=s+1}^{t}\varepsilon_{u}^{\min(a,b)-\frac{d}{2}}\right)\>.

Indeed, first recall that
\|g_{\delta,N}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\Phi\|_{\infty}\leq M
so that
I_{s}\leq MM_{s}
.
Let now
s<u\leq t
, using (
32
) and the Lipschitz estimate (
7
) for
g_{\delta,N}
(between
x_{j}^{u}
and
x_{q}^{u-1}
satisfying
\left|x_{j}^{u}-x_{q}^{u-1}\right|\leq 3\varepsilon_{u-1}\leq 9\varepsilon_{u}
instead of
x
and
x_{j}^{t}
) we obtain the following recurrence relation:

\displaystyle I_{u}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\sum^{m_{u-1}}_{q=1}\sum_{j\in I_{q,u}}\left(g_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{u})-g_{\delta,N}(x_{q}^{u-1})\right)\>\left(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(A_{j}^{u})-\mu(A_{j}^{u})\right)\Bigg|+\Bigg|\sum^{m_{u-1}}_{q=1}g_{\delta,N}(x_{q}^{u-1})\>\underbrace{\sum_{j\in I_{q,u}}\left(\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(A_{j}^{u})-\mu(A_{j}^{u})\right)}_{=\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(A_{q}^{u-1})-\mu(A_{q}^{u-1})}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{m_{u-1}}_{q=1}\sum_{j\in I_{q,u}}M\left(\varepsilon_{u}+\left|\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x_{j}^{u})-\theta_{\delta,N}^{\bf Y}(x_{q}^{u-1})\right|+\left\|\sigma_{\delta,N}(x_{j}^{u})-\sigma_{\delta,N}(x_{q}^{u-1})\right\|\right)\left|\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(A_{j}^{u})-\mu(A_{j}^{u})\right|+I_{u-1}\>.

We can now take the supremum with respect to
f\in{\rm BL}
and use the independence of
{\bf X}
and
\left({\bf Y},\tilde{\bf Y},{\bf Z}\right)
to infer the following relation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}I_{u}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in{\rm BL}}I_{u-1}\right]+M\sum^{m_{u-1}}_{q=1}\sum_{j\in I_{q,u}}\mathbb{E}_{\bf X}\left[\left|\mu_{N}^{\bf X}(A_{j}^{u})-\mu(A_{j}^{u})\right|\right]\times
